  [G10.4] Vulnerability has a material impact on the recommendation (overlay)
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report does not assert any vulnerability impact on the recommendation. No statement appears in the canonical model at `/vulnerability/perClient[]/impactOnRecommendation[]` or `/vulnerability/foreseeableHarms[]`.
    File:     The case file contains evidence of limited financial knowledge and investment understanding. [P2] shows the client has 'No understanding / knowledge' of stocks and shares and 'Very little understanding / knowledge' of pension planning. [P15] states the client 'describe yourself as having very little understanding or knowledge when it comes to investing' with 'no understanding of unit trusts, stocks and shares, or enterprise investment schemes.' [P16] notes the client's risk profile as 'Cautious Balanced' and states 'you may take some time to make investment decisions and tend to be somewhat anxious about those decisions.' The Risk Profile report [P15] documents questionnaire responses indicating limited investment experience. [P14] records that the client 'did not have time to complete an ATRQ today, so have sent him an email version to complete himself on Defaqto,' suggesting potential comprehension or engagement concerns. No formal vulnerability assessment, health issues, life event triggers, support needs, third-party authority, communication adjustments, or capacity concerns are documented in the file.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report does not address the material finding that the client has very limited investment knowledge (no understanding of stocks and shares, very little understanding of pensions) yet is being recommended investment products including an ISA and GIA.
      - The report does not explicitly connect the client's Cautious Balanced risk profile, which indicates anxiety about investment decisions and need for time to decide, to any adjustment in communication, support, or suitability reasoning.
      - The report does not document whether the client's limited understanding of investments and decision-making anxiety materially impact the appropriateness of the recommendation or require additional protective measures.
      - No formal vulnerability assessment, foreseeability of harm, or support needs assessment appears anywhere in the case file despite clear indicators of low financial capability.
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Formal vulnerability assessment or capability evaluation, Documentation of whether limited investment knowledge materially impacts suitability of recommended products, Assessment of comprehension support needs given decision-making anxiety, Any explicit analysis of how the client's 'very little understanding' of pension planning affects the pension recommendation, Documentation of whether third-party involvement, trusted contact, or communication adjustments are required
    The requirement asks whether the report adequately supports any assertion that vulnerability has a material impact on the recommendation. The canonical model contains no such assertion — the extraction found no values at the specified vulnerability paths. However, the absence of an assertion does not resolve the overlay requirement; the question is whether vulnerability was assessed and, if found, properly reflected in the recommendation reasoning. The case file shows multiple indicators of limited financial capability: the client explicitly states 'very little understanding or knowledge' of investing (P2, P15); scores indicate 'No understanding' of stocks and shares and enterprise schemes (P2); and the risk profile describes the client as anxious about decisions and needing time to decide (P16). The client also failed to complete the attitude-to-risk questionnaire in person, requiring it to be sent by email (P14). These are Category D (risk and capability) indicators under FG21/1, not vulnerabilities in the PRIN 2A sense that would trigger special safeguards, but they are capability and comprehension concerns that should be assessed and accounted for in the suitability reasoning. The report does acknowledge 'very little understanding or knowledge when it comes to investing' in [P16] and describes the Cautious Balanced profile as suited to those 'who do not find financial matters particularly easy to understand' (P15). However, the report does not explicitly analyse whether this limited understanding materially affects the suitability of the specific recommendations (pension switch, ISA, GIA) or whether additional communication support or protective measures are required. No vulnerability assessment tool, support needs analysis, or foreseeability-of-harm framework appears in the case. The extraction's own summary states 'overall confidence 0.87' and notes ambiguities but no recorded vulnerabilities in the structured sense. Given the instruction that the overlay applies to every case and that where no vulnerability assessment exists the finding is that 'the overlay was never applied,' the material issue here is that capability and comprehension concerns have been identified but not formally assessed for material impact on the recommendation. This is a compliance concern: the report acknowledges low understanding but does not demonstrate how this has been weighed in reaching the recommendation.

  [G10.5] Vulnerabilities recorded in the file and in the report
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate
    Report:   The suitability report asserts hasVulnerability = 'No' under /vulnerability/perClient[]. No vulnerability categories, characteristics, or assessment date are recorded in the extracted canonical model. The report does not elaborate on any vulnerability screening or decision to mark the client as having no vulnerabilities.
    File:     The Fact Find (P1, P2, P7) records no specific vulnerability indicators, support needs declarations, or requests for adjustments. Communication preference is recorded as 'No Preference' for delivery method (P1). Accessibility format requirement is 'No Requirement' (P1). The Data Protection statement (P7) confirms consent for processing but contains no notation of vulnerability or special support needs. Notes (P8, P9) record employment and retirement planning details but do not flag any vulnerabilities. No Power of Attorney, trusted contact, representative, or third-party involvement is documented. The client completed a telephone meeting on 06/08/2025 with no other person present (P7). No health conditions, capacity concerns, literacy issues, or comprehension difficulties are mentioned anywhere in the file. The ATRQ was sent by email for self-completion (P1) because 'Client did not have time to complete an ATRQ today', indicating a scheduling convenience rather than a support need.
    The report's assertion that hasVulnerability = 'No' is consistent with the absence of any vulnerability indicators, support needs, or special characteristics recorded in the case file. No contradicting evidence exists: the Fact Find contains no ticked boxes or notations indicating vulnerabilities, no accessibility requirements are noted, no third-party arrangements are documented, and no health or capacity concerns appear in the notes or advisor observations. The client's age (55, born 06/01/1970 per P14) is not a vulnerability marker under the guard rules. The email dispatch of the ATRQ reflects administrative convenience, not a support need. Communication preferences show standard contact methods with no recorded preference for accessible formats or special delivery. The absence of any vulnerability flag in the canonical model aligns with the absence of evidence in the supporting documents. However, the extraction metadata notes that /vulnerability/perClient[]/categories[], /vulnerability/perClient[]/characteristics[], and /vulnerability/perClient[]/assessedOn are absent from the extracted model. This means the extraction did not capture any underlying vulnerability screening or assessment process, if one occurred. The report's silence on *how* or *when* the 'No' determination was made creates a gap: the file does not evidence that a vulnerability assessment was performed at all, only that no vulnerabilities are recorded in the outcome. Under PRIN 2A.2.1R and FG21/1, advisers must assess vulnerability even where none is found. The report does not articulate this assessment or the reasoning behind the 'No' conclusion. The file contains no memo, checklist, or advisor note documenting that vulnerability was screened and ruled out. This represents an absence of supporting documentation for the report's assertion, not a contradiction of it.
